\title{Universe24: a local integer law of nature and what follows from it}
\author{Alon Gonen\\

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
One piecewise-linear map on an integer torus is the law. The state of the universe is a vector of
bounded integers on the GameBoard; the law is one
map applied at every Node at every interval, per component with a rate
and a wall: translate, count the walls it holds (capped at one for the
drive), subtract them; every wall counted is an event. Its rates are constant for a message in
transit and bilinear in the state for a body reading what arrives; the
one read-out that deletes is the click, one comparison of a birth phase
against a record's integer square. From this law, its
tables and the declared families follow: Gauss's law exactly, Newton's and
Coulomb's inverse square, the rows' pace $c = 1/\sqrt3$
Links per interval (a body's pace not bounded by it as declared), the click's weight a positive
quadratic form of power $2$ under the splitter's conservation, Tsirelson's bound as a limit, the
uncertainty relation exact on the phase circle and Poisson's equation; in form, a bound set's mass; and, in the limit,
Doppler, Young's spacing, Bohr's condition (unchecked) and Kepler's laws (pinned, not run).
What does not follow: Newton's constant and the masses are inputs;
Einstein's field equation is not reached; the Lorentz factor is not in
the law as declared, which predicts an unslowed clock in motion; the
covariant readings beside it give it on paper. Twenty-two
dimensionless readings meet nature: five pass
(four replicated, one a pin, unrun), twelve fail (seven the law's,
four pinned, one an input), one passes under the
clock's word assumed, the age moment, and fails under the presence
word; two bound a parameter, two await a run. One prediction: $S = 181/64$ at the powers of two $512$ to $8192$, run and replicated at $512$ and $4096$, inside Poh et al. at $1.1$ standard errors; at $16384$ the rule gives $5793/2048$, a function of the grains, not a constant. Every claim is measured, proved, assumed or
open.
\end{abstract}

\section{Introduction}

\paragraph{One law.} This paper states one law for a universe of bounded
integers and reports, with one source per number and one of four labels
per claim (measured in a recorded run, proved from the definitions,
assumed, or open), what follows from it and what does not. The law is
one map applied at every Node of a cubic GameBoard at every interval:
each component of the state is an accumulator with a declared rate and a
declared wall, translated by its rate, counted in walls (capped at one
for the drive), reduced by the walls it holds, and every wall counted
is an event. Nothing else happens. Where a component's rate is a constant of the world (a
message in transit) the map has a closed form and its consequences are
computed before any run; where the rate is a function of what arrives (a
body reading the messages at its Node) the map is iterated and its
continuum limit is a differential equation. One comparison is read out,
the click, and it is the only place a quantity leaves the state.
Section~\ref{sec:law} states it once as Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}), names its
symbols, lists the six operations that make its rates and walls, and
gives its immediate special cases before any derivation; the paper
refers back to Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) thereafter. What is new here, it
seems, is not any formula recovered but the form of the law that
recovers them, one map of bounded integers with no real number
anywhere, with its supplied structures stored as integers (the tables,
the pair's joint contraction; the energy's square is the covariant readings'), and the ledger
beside it that marks each recovery; whether
that form is nature's is what the rows of Section~\ref{sec:nature} and
the one prediction of Section~\ref{sec:differs} put to measurement
(the precedents of the great papers, read for this \cite{precedents}).

\paragraph{What is derived and what is put in.} Part~I states the law:
the state, the map, its two blocks, the read-out, and the inputs (the
grain of the computation, the table of families with the world's width,
the initial state). Part~II is one table: every formula of known physics
that the law reaches in the limit and confirms in a recorded run, every
constant it takes as an input, every formula it does not reach, and
every place where it gives a different law, stated as a row that a
measurement can refute; each entry names the section of the derivation
that carries it and the run or the pin that checks it, and in this
version the table stands on those sections and the register, its rows
to be written out one by one as each passes review; its written-out
rows are Newton and Coulomb, the delay field, the dark sector and the
lattice Gleason with the uncertainty relation, and
Section~\ref{sec:qm} places the founding formulas of quantum mechanics
one by one. Part~III is the
measurement chapter: the click model exactly, the pace of its rows and
its rounding, four theorems, and the runs of one quantum, a pair, three
quanta and a gate, recorded before the text. Part~IV states the
continuum limits that return and those that do not, the decision taken
on Lorentz, and the literature.

\paragraph{Method.} Equations first, runs as confirmation. Every formula
is derived from the definitions in a derivation document of the archive
\cite{derivations}, cited here by section; every registered run is cited from the
experiments register \cite{register} with its world, its expectation
written before the run and its source fingerprint (the three crowd
runs of Section~\ref{sec:delay} from their design notes, their register
entries drafted and not registered); every number of the
manuscript is mapped to its source in a table beside it \cite{checks}.
A formula whose confirming run has not been made is stated as a pin, not
as a result. Every formula is reached in three steps recorded per
formula in the appendix: the pin (an expectation derived from the closed
form alone and written to the register before the run, or the statement
that none exists), the limit (the
closed form against the classical formula, with the order and the error
term where the derivation states them) and the run (a world that
declares families, grain and initial state and no formula, read by a
tool that compares with the pin); so the derivation does not depend on
the formula it reaches. In one line: the infinite limit derives the
form, the run confirms the number, and the pin only orders that the
first be written before the second; the known formula is what remains
when the grain vanishes, and the finite-grain departures are the law's
signature, not noise \cite{log}. The rows added in this revision are
stated to the six-point standard of the papers that derive a continuum
law from one lattice rule \cite{fhp1986,wilson1974,qian1992}: the rule,
the limit taken, the order of the expansion, the symmetry needed, the
error term and the check against a value pinned first (\cite{derivations},
section 21.5; Table~\ref{tab:recordc}); the earlier rows carry the order
and the error term where the derivation states them. The GameBoard, the lattice of Nodes with six Ports on
which the one map is applied per interval, is the instrument of every
table entry and not the subject of this paper. The archive will be
cited at a version \cite{zenodo}.

\paragraph{Notation.} Every formula of this paper is written in
physics' letters with physics' meanings, and the law's own constants
carry names a physicist does not read as charge, action or Newton's
constant: the grains of the lattice are $N$ with a subscript naming
what each one counts. Table~\ref{tab:notation} is the one symbol table
(the owner's order, record 362 \cite{log}; the program's proposal
and its decision on it \cite{notation}); every symbol
is named in English at its first use, a scalar plain, a vector in bold
lowercase, a matrix, a tensor or an operator in bold uppercase, and a
world key or a code identifier in code font, never a formula symbol.

\part{The formula}\label{part:formula}

\section{The law}\label{sec:law}

\paragraph{The postulates.} What is assumed is listed before what is
derived, so that a reader can see which is which; everything after
Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) is claimed as its consequence or marked otherwise in
the standing column of Table~\ref{tab:consequences}
\cite{postulates,highlights,lawvector}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(P1)] \emph{Bounded integers.} Every quantity of the state is a
bounded integer and every operation on it is integer arithmetic; there
is no real number, no root and no floating point at run time, and the
roundings declared at load are P7's constants; a world whose counts
would exceed the declared bounds is refused at load where the static
budget covers it and refused loudly at the step that would exceed a
bound otherwise; nothing wraps or clamps.
\item[(P2)] \emph{The GameBoard.} Space is a cubic array of Nodes, each
joined to six neighbours by Links through directional Ports; a Node's
complete local information is its NodeState, and time is a sequence of
intervals.
\item[(P3)] \emph{The six operations.} The rates and walls of the map
are made of the six operations enumerated below Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) and
of nothing else; there is no seventh.
\item[(P4)] \emph{Locality.} A rule reads its own record and the
crowd, the arrivals at its Node and its six neighbouring Nodes, already
delivered (LOCALITY-1), and nothing kept at a Node beyond the events
there: no register, no remainder, no draw; the work and the storage per
Node are fixed for a fixed neighbourhood.
\item[(P5)] \emph{One Link per interval.} No row or body crosses more
than one Link per interval (a body one Link on one axis, the first
whose rule fires): the causal bound, from which the octahedron and the
bound $1/\sqrt3$ on an isotropic pace follow
(Proposition~\ref{prop:pace}). The rows sit at that bound by the
flight's declared wall $T_D$ (P9); given the wall, $c = 1/\sqrt3$ is
the flight operator's norm (the derivation's 24.1, row 9).
\item[(P6)] \emph{The click.} A record is read out once, by one
comparison, at the click; it is the one step that deletes. The click
of a pair is one gather of one record from both settings, the law's
one non-local operation (Section~\ref{sec:causal}), whose state (the
record's live count and its offers) is kept in the apparatus's list
and not at a Node. A
measurement is a detector's reading, a click, a record's moments or an
external thing's reading, as the world file declares; only a
measurement is compared with nature.
\item[(P7)] \emph{The grains, constants of the law.} The circle $\Nphi$, the
pace's grain $N_l$, the sphere of directions $N_D$, the birth wheel $N_u$,
the fan's grain $N_\theta$, the clock's pair, and the roundings declared at
load, among them the circle's rounding at $1/256$ (record 328
\cite{log}).
\item[(P8)] \emph{The physical inputs.} The family table, per family
the content (the mass number), the cost $h$ per phase step, the charge
per unit of content, the strong column, the lifetime, the phase rate
and the hand (its instance, every family the register declares with
its values, in Appendix~\ref{sec:families}); the world's width $N_w$, the
push per unit of content per unit of flow, which carries what physics calls Newton's constant; and
the state and the apparatus of a world (the box with its boundaries,
the bodies with their momenta and contents, the lamps, the detectors,
the splitters, the gates).
\item[(P9)] \emph{Rules chosen among few (the ledger's rows 7, 20 and
21, and the clock's word, which the ledger does not yet carry).} The flight is blind
to the crowd: a row's rate in transit is constant, its wall the
direction's $T_D$ (the alternative, a wall reading the age moment, is
optical-v1, a hypothesis beside the law); a body's drive is the flight
at the fraction its momentum earns (form B, decided and in build, not
on main; Newton's limit fixes its small-momentum form); and the
collision's permutation is the cyclic shift (conservation forces a
bijection of the slots, the shift is the choice). A fourth rule is the
clock's word: the law as built counts, per table entry, the presence
of the rows at a body's Node by default and their age moment on an
entry that reads the age (\cite{beamlaw}, section 3); the owner's word
of 2026-09-21 (its record to follow) makes the age moment, the
potential's form $M/r$, the law's word, the engine's default to
follow. It is an assumption, not a derivation: the alternative, the
presence, $M/r^2$, reads a flat field at two distances on the
GameBoard (series T) where nature's clocks between two heights follow
the potential.
\item[(P10)] \emph{One imported member of a derived family.} The
click's weight is forced to the family $\sum_j c_j|\sigma_j(f)|^2$
(Theorem~\ref{th:gleason}); Born's member, $c_1 = 1$ and the other
$c_j = 0$, is the one law imported from quantum mechanics, a
postulate.
\end{enumerate}
The list is the derivation's inputs ledger (24.1 \cite{derivations}):
P1 to P6 the law's form, P7 its grains, P8 its inputs and its
apparatus, P9 its rules chosen among few, P10 its one imported member.
What is not in this list is not assumed, and what the paper
derives from Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) under it, the bilinear rate of a
body's reading included (24.2: forced by two additivities that are
postulates already, over the arriving rows and over the reader's
units, the moment's order the one choice, the columns' values P8's),
is marked DERIVED in
Table~\ref{tab:consequences}, the rest INPUT, HYPOTHESIS,
DIFFERENT, NOT REACHED or OPEN (the postulates themselves are P1 to
P10, no row of the table).

The law is one map $\bPhi$ applied at every Node of the GameBoard
at every interval, per component $s$ of the state, each with its own
rate $r$ and wall $d$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:map}
s \leftarrow s + r, \qquad e \leftarrow \operatorname{sign}(s)\,\min\!\left(\lfloor |s|/d \rfloor,\ a\right), \qquad s \leftarrow s - e\,d .
\end{equation}
Here $s$ is one accumulator of the state, a bounded integer on a circle
or a line; $r$ its declared rate, signed, a constant of the world for a
message in transit and a reading of what arrives for a body; $d$ its
declared wall, a positive integer; $a$ the component's cap, $1$ where a
cap is declared and $a = \infty$ where none is (the whole part; the
primitive's \texttt{at\_most} $= 0$); and $e$ the event count: every
unit of $e$ is an event (a Link crossed, a phase step taken, a count
completed, a birth, a push), and nothing else happens; a reversed rate
first cancels what was accumulated the other way. For a count without
cap the third step is the division toward zero with its signed remainder kept, the Euclidean division for $s \ge 0$;
under the cap the residual above the wall is kept and fires one event
at each following interval until spent. The cap $a = 1$ is the
drive's alone, one Link per interval (form B's directional drive the
same); every other count takes the whole part and can fire several
events in one interval, the turn, the owed count, the release, the
lamp, the push, so that a rate of $5$ against a wall of $2$ fires $0,
2, 5, 7, 10$ events over the intervals, as the owner's high-rate runs
read (record 340 \cite{log}). The $0/1$ form $e \leftarrow [s \ge d]$,
one event bit per interval, holds exactly where the rate never exceeds
the wall: the flight ($2S_1N_l \le 2T_D$, at most one Link per
interval), the drive by its cap and with the sign ($e \in \{-1, 0,
1\}$), the birth wheel (the rate $1$ against
$N_u$) and a clock whose rate is below its wall (the derivation's section
0 \cite{derivations}). The rates and walls of every
component are made of six operations and nothing else
\cite{highlights,lawvector}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the translation of an accumulator by its rate (the flight, the
phase, the age, every count);
\item the multiplication by a declared integer matrix or bilinear form
(a split, a rotation; a coupling, the click's Gram matrix);
\item the addition in the group ring $\Z[\Z_{\Nphi}]$ (the merge, where
opposite phases cancel);
\item the permutation of the joint state (a collision, a gate);
\item the evaluation of the record at the $\Nphi$th root of unity, $\Z[\Z_{\Nphi}]
\to \Z[\zeta_{\Nphi}]$ ($\Nphi$ the phase circle's width), and its norm (the
click's one quadratic step);
\item the division toward zero with the remainder kept (the Euclidean
division for a non-negative accumulator), and the
comparison (the carry that is the event; the cap; the threshold).
\end{enumerate}
Two places are not linear and there is no third: the carry, a bijection
$s \mapsto (e, s - ed)$ with $|s - ed| < d$ and the remainder's sign that of $s$, which loses nothing, and the click's threshold,
the one comparison read out per record, whose remainder is not read:
the one place the law deletes by design. The cap and the sign are
operation 6's comparison, $\min(\lfloor |s|/d \rfloor, 1) = [|s| \ge
d]$; the capped carry is the injection $s \mapsto (e, s - ed)$, which
loses nothing either, so there is still no third place.

\paragraph{The special cases that follow at once.} Each is
Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) with one rate, wall or content set to zero, or one
wall multiplied by a factor; each is stated in one line with the place
that carries it \cite{derivations,lawvector}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item A row is a body of zero content: under the drive on the
momentum's direction (decided and in build, not on main; the vector form's 4.3) the
drive at $N_l N_w M = 0$ is the flight itself, the flight's ratio $S_1
N_l/T_D$ of Links per interval, hence $c = 1/\sqrt3$ ($N_l$ the
flight's grain, $N_w$ the world's width, $M$ the content, $T_D$ the
direction's wall, $S_1$ its Manhattan length; Section~\ref{sec:model},
Proposition~\ref{prop:pace}). Under the drive as built, per axis, the
case gives one Link per interval on the axis, the causal bound; and
that drive has an axis preference, the first axis whose rule fires
making the step, $x$ before $y$ before $z$ when two fire in one
interval, so that equal momentum on two axes moved a body $26$ Nodes on
$x$ and none on $y$; and a body's pace is not bounded by $c$: a fast
body advanced $0.925$ Nodes per interval against the rows' $0.582$ on
an axis, the cap being one Link per interval in all (the engine's one
step per interval), $T_D/N_l = 1.72$ times the rows' pace on a heading
($\sqrt3\,c = 1.73c$) (the owner's eight runs on main, record 340
\cite{log}; the derivation's 24.3, rows 21 and 22, both refuted on
main as declared and closed under form B's directional drive, in
build, and the covariant readings' $pc^2/E \le 1/\sqrt3$).
\item A free family is declared at zero cost, $h = 0$, with the table's
reading in place of absorption: its rows push their reader and take no
recoil at birth, so for it the third law is a symmetry between two
readers, not a conservation message by message (statement I5 below;
the derivation's 3.3).
\item A pair at zero turns a rule off: a suspension of $0$ owes no
interval and nothing slows the clock (statement I4; the derivation's
4.3); a world key absent is no meeting and no optical rule (the
derivation's 5.4).
\item The push is bilinear, $\mathrm{push} = M\,\mathbf a$ on the
gravity column with $\mathbf a$ the label flow of the arriving rows
(the charge column adds $\rho_A\rho_B M\,\mathbf a$), and the
acceleration at small momentum is $\mathrm{push}/(N_l N_w M)$: at zero
flow no push; the content cancels in the ratio, the equivalence
principle (the derivation's 3.3, 3.4).
\item A wall times a factor changes one thing wherever $T_D$ is a wall:
the growing wall $2T_D\,a$, with $a$ the wall's scale rising at the
rate $H$ per interval, is the expansion (the derivation's 15); the
optical wall $2T_D(d_s + f n_s \mathcal A)$, with $n_s/d_s$ the clock's
suspension pair (the owed count's rate), $\mathcal A$ the age moment of
the rows at the Node (Section~\ref{sec:delay}) and $f$ the declared
factor, is the rows reading the crowd (optical-v1, designed and not
built \cite{grrows}).
\item A family declared without a circle is phase-less: it turns never
and clicks by no phase; a rate of zero steps per Link is the case with
the circle kept (the family table's declaration \cite{highlights}).
\item The remainder is kept at every division but the click's, the one
read-out, so nothing else is lost and the floor of every rate is zero
(the charge column's remainder under its accumulator): no rounding of
the weak field, no acceleration below which the law departs from itself
(Section~\ref{sec:dark}).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{From the Node to the group, the octahedron and the pace.}
Everything geometric in the law follows from one Node and its six
Ports, $\pm x$, $\pm y$, $\pm z$, and from one Link per interval. The maps
of the six Ports onto themselves that keep opposite Ports opposite are
the signed permutations of the three axes: $3!$ orderings of the axes
times $2^3$ choices of sign, $48$ in all, the symmetry group of the cube
and of the octahedron (Theorem~\ref{th:group}). The determinant of a
signed permutation is $\pm 1$, so it splits the $48$ into two halves of
$24$: the rotations, of determinant $+1$, which are the permutations of
the cube's four body diagonals; and the improper maps, of determinant
$-1$, the reflections with the central inversion among them. The hand
of a row is that determinant made a column of the state: one
pseudoscalar bit that a map $g$ sends to $\det(g)$ times itself, so a
world with a hand reads which half a map belongs to (series P's parity
worlds with an axis read exactly the $24$ improper maps and none of the
$24$ rotations, a hand without an axis being mirror-equal under all
$48$; records 142, 226 and 231, and the owner's direction, record 334
\cite{log}). The same six Ports bound
the pace: the Nodes one interval away are the six neighbours at the
ends of the Links, the vertices of the octahedron $|x| + |y| + |z| \le
1$, the causal front of one interval; a pace that is the same in every
direction and crosses at most one Link per interval is at most the
radius of that octahedron's inscribed sphere, $1/\sqrt3$ Links per
interval, the sphere touching the eight faces on the cube's diagonals
(Proposition~\ref{prop:pace}, Figure~\ref{fig:octahedron}); that the
rows' pace $c$ sits at that bound and not below is the flight's
declared wall $T_D$, the design's third statement and a rule chosen
among few (P9), and given the wall the value is the flight operator's
norm, attained exactly on the body diagonals (the derivation's 24.1,
row 9). The group, the front and the bound are one figure: $48$ and
$24$ are counts of it and $1/\sqrt3$ its bound, none a constant of the
law; the constants are $\Nphi$, $N_l$, $N_D$, $N_u$, $N_\theta$ the fan's grain, the
clock's pair and the roundings declared at load, below (the $48$ one by
one in Figure~\ref{fig:the48}).

\paragraph{What follows from Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}), in order.} After the
six operations and the special cases above, the laws follow in this
order, each from a named operation, and each was reached by the three
steps of the Method: the closed form in the limit first, the pin
written from it, the run after (the appendix's derivation record,
Tables~\ref{tab:recorda} and~\ref{tab:recordb}, holds the pin and the
reading of every row).
\begin{enumerate}
\item Gauss's law, exactly: the release (a count against a wall, the
carry, operation 6) and the walk (a translation, operation 1) keep the
flux through any closed surface equal to the enclosed release
(Section~\ref{sec:newton}; the derivation's 3.1); reached by the limit and confirmed by the flux
$1.0000$ through a square (series C).
\item Newton's and Coulomb's inverse square, in the shell mean: the
push is bilinear in the content and the arriving flow (operation 2),
the flow through a shell is the release over its Node count
(Section~\ref{sec:newton}; 3.3, 3.4); reached by the limit, the slopes
and the ratio $-1$, $-1/4$ pinned and read in the plane (series C), the
space run not made.
\item The Doppler of a moving reader, from the crossing count: a
reader counts the rows it crosses, one Euclidean division (operation
6), $1 \pm v/c$ on the axis (2.2, 2.7), and the source's Doppler $1/(1
\mp v/c)$ from the same count (2.5), read on series G2's coasting
stars; the receiver's reached by the limit and by the rule's tests, its
registered run under the rule not made.
\item Poisson's equation, the retarded wave equation and the clock's
redshift at first order, from the age moment of the rows at a Node
(Section~\ref{sec:delay}; 5); reached by the limit, the $1/r$ form read
on the shells (series E).
\item Born's rule as a limit, the lattice Gleason and the uncertainty
relation, from the evaluation at the root of unity and its norm
(operation 5; Section~\ref{sec:gleason}; 6, 22); reached by the limit
($1/\Nphi$ per cell, $1/(2\Nphi)$ per cumulative rung) and the pinned counts $64/0$, $63/1$ (series L), the form
proved.
\item Young's spacing, from the fan's angular measure and the click
(7.1); reached in the limit of every direction, the clicks under the
birth wheel at $4096$ births read against their rungs (L2b).
\item Bohr's condition in form, from the turn under the action and the
push (7.2); reached in form, no orbit closed on the register (series
H), the levels open (the energies not reached).
\item The information law and the entropy, from the one read-out that
is not a bijection (6.1, 9, 14); the bound proved, the numbers host
readings of registered runs.
\item The expansion as a growing wall and its redshift, from a wall
multiplied by a rising factor (15); the form reached, Milne's $q = 0$
pinned and read inside ($-0.108$ coasting, series G2), nature's $-0.53$
a FAIL (Section~\ref{sec:nature}).
\item The masses in form (19; series R); Kepler's laws in the limit and
Compton's formula on the exact square, both pinned and not run (21.2,
rows 58 and 59, their blocks in 21.5); each in
Table~\ref{tab:consequences} with its status.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{The state.} The GameBoard is a box of Nodes in $\Z^3$, each
joined to six neighbours by Links through six Ports; an axis may be
periodic. Time is the interval $t \in \Z$. The state at an interval is a
vector $\mathbf s$ of bounded integers on a product of circles and lines,
and it is the multiset of the messages in transit (the rows) and the
bodies (the measured events), and nothing else: a Node holds no
register, remainder or draw. A row carries its Node, its direction (a
primitive integer vector on whose digital line it walks), its age, its
phase on the circle $\Z_{\Nphi}$, its number (its family), its amount, its
content per unit, and under a record its identity, label, multiplicity
and birth phase. A body carries its Node, its content $M$, its momentum
vector $\mathbf p$ and its table of counts, one accumulator per count
(the fields per kind and the six verbs on them, F4 in
Appendix~\ref{sec:families}).

\paragraph{The components of Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}).} The ladder's rungs
$\rung_k$ of Part~III are walls of this kind. The components and their $(r, d)$ \cite{derivations}: a row's Manhattan count (the rate $2S_1N_l$,
the wall $2T_D$, started at $T_D$; $D$ the direction vector, $S_1$ its
Manhattan length, $T_D = \lfloor\sqrt{3|D|^2N_l^2}\rfloor$ its
resolution, $N_l$ the flight's grain), with three axis deficits choosing
the axis of each Link; a row's phase (an integer per Link, or a declared
rational $\nu$ of steps per interval, written $n/d$ in Part~III, its
whole part read modulo $\Nphi$); a row's age (the rate $1$, no wall); a
body's drive on each axis (the rate $|p_a|$, the wall $N_l N_w M +
|p_a|$, $M$ the content and $N_w$ the world's width), whose speed $p/(N_l N_w M + p)$ saturates at one Link per
interval, above the rows' pace (the drive as the flight operator at a
fraction, capped at $c$, is decided and in build, not on main); a body's counts (the
turn, the owed count, the release, the lamp, the push per column, each a
rate against a wall); a record's birth phase (the rate $1$ per birth,
the wall $\Nphi$; the birth wheel, a lamp's declared rate $r_w$ per birth against the wall $N_u$, one row of the lamp's counts table, giving each record its coordinate $u = \mathrm{ordinal}\cdot r_w \bmod N_u$ on the ladder ($r_w$ the wheel's rate, the ordinal the record's count from $0$, the birth phase $u \bmod \Nphi$); $[2531, 4096]$ on the registered two-slit world, the rate the nearest odd integer to $0.618 \times 4096$ \cite{beamlaw}); the exact phase at the click, the click's one Euclidean division with its remainder kept, from the row's two counts, the phase per interval and the Links made, at the exact time of its last Link \cite{beamlaw}; and
the click's ladder, one comparison read once. Beside the six
operations stand the roundings declared at load, each a constant of the
law, among them the tables of the circle at $1/256$, the unit vector of
a direction to the nearest integer vector, $T_D$ by the integer square
root, the click's rung to the nearest integer and the fan's weights at
the grain $N_\theta$ \cite{lawvector}.

\paragraph{The two blocks.} $\bPhi$ is linear where its rates are
constants of the world and feeds back where a rate is a function of what
arrives. The linear block is the rows in flight: the flight, the phase
and the age at constant rates, the merge, the integer matrices of a
split, a rotation or a gate, and the evaluation at the click. Every
component of it has the closed form of its unwrapped count, $\lfloor s_0 + rt \rfloor$ (the accumulator itself is that count's remainder by the wall), so
a row's state at any interval is a formula of its birth and its age, and
the limits of Part~II are limits of that formula. The feedback block is
the bodies: the push, whose rate is the reader's content times the flow
of the arriving rows (bilinear in the state); the owed count, whose rate
is what the body reads (the presence, or the age moment); the turn and
the release, whose rate is the content; the drive, whose rate is the
momentum the push changes. These have no closed form; they are iterated,
and their continuum limit is the differential equation $d\mathbf s/dt =
\mathbf r(\mathbf s)$. The exact word for the whole map is therefore
piecewise-linear in the rows with bilinear rates in the bodies
\cite{derivations}. What each rule reads is audited rule by rule
\cite{beamlaw}: of the $23$ rules in force at the audited commit, $13$
read the crowd (everything at the Node but the rule's own number, and
the six neighbouring Nodes) and $10$ read only their own record: the
walk, the escape at a face, the click's choice, the re-emission, the
rotation, the turn, the birth, the release, the border lifetime and the
decay's timing.

\paragraph{The read-out.} The click reads one comparison and nothing else
leaves the rows (a detector's other readings, the counts, the flow and
the moments of the rows at its Node, read and delete nothing): for a
record whose rows have all ended, the apparatus
sums the rows' integer phasors per cell through the tables, squares the
sums (the one quadratic step of the law, where the Born rule enters,
forced in form by Section~\ref{sec:gleason} and free in its constants),
lays the cells on a ladder by those squared sums, and compares the
record's coordinate $u$, the wheel's, with the ladder's rungs; the cell
whose rung $u$ falls under is the click, and the record is done. Between a birth and a
click every step of the linear block is injective given the apparatus's
record (Theorem~\ref{th:bijection}); the click is the one deletion.

\paragraph{The law in five statements.} (I1) A message is a row: created
at exactly one event, a self-creation of a body, ended at exactly one
event, a read that absorbs it, an escape or the border of its lifetime;
between them in transit, and nothing else exists. (I2) A Node transmits
per interval the rows that step out of it, one Port and one Link each at
the flight's pace, holding nothing back and copying nothing. (I3) A body
receives the rows of other numbers that reach it and emits only at its
self-creations. (I4) A clock's rate is the reciprocal of one plus what
it reads: a body's clock counts its self-creations and owes after each
an interval count proportional to what it reads of the messages (their
presence or their age moment, P9), so it is slowed by messages and by
nothing else. (I5) The balance: every message
released is on exactly one line of the books at every interval,
released equals in transit plus absorbed plus escaped plus cancelled,
per family, in amount and in content, exact at every interval; a paid
message carries its label out of its emitter at birth and into its
reader at its end, so for paid messages momentum is conserved and the
third law holds message by message, while a free message (a field's row)
takes no recoil at birth and pushes its reader by its label times the
reader's content, so for free messages the third law is a symmetry
between two readers, exact only while both read the same number of each
other's messages \cite{derivations}.

\paragraph{What is put in.} The grain of the computation (P7),
constants of the law and not of the world; the table of families with
the world's width (P8); and the state and the apparatus (P8)
\cite{highlights}. The circle's rounding among the grain is an input
by declaration, with the
standing of $\Nphi$ and $N_l$, and not an approximation to be removed, and
it is kept (record 328 \cite{log}): the tables of $\cos$ and $\sin$ of
every phase step, scaled by $256$ and rounded to the nearest integer,
computed once at load from integer series, law data and not a register
at a Node, read at the click through the Gram matrix
$\mathbf G = \mathbf E^{\mathsf T}\mathbf E$ ($\mathbf E$ the $2 \times \Nphi$
matrix of the tables, $\mathbf G$ the click's Gram matrix, not the fan's
grain $N_\theta$ above; Section~\ref{sec:gleason}) and, at $2\Nphi$, at the label
rotation (the half-angle tables, Section~\ref{sec:model}). The
declaration has precedents in signal processing, where a rounding of
the circle is taken as the definition and kept: the integer transform
of H.264/AVC, whose cosines, replaced by small integers, are the
standard, so that every decoder gives the same bits \cite{malvar2003};
the fixed-point FFT, whose error from the rounded factors is bounded
above and below, the rounding kept \cite{welch1969}; the stored waveform
table read at a rate, the digital instrument's oscillator
\cite{mathews1963}; and the computed hologram's phase quantized to $\Nphi$
equally spaced levels (their $\Nphi$), the precedent for the finite circle
$\Z_{\Nphi}$ itself \cite{goodman1970}. In the program's words: ``if the law is right, the input to the
real world is the family table, the initial state and one grain; $c$,
$h$ and $G$ are units there as here'' \cite{highlights}.

\paragraph{The symmetries.} The law is invariant under the translations
of the box and of the interval, and under the $48$ signed permutations
of the axes, the group of the octahedron of the six Ports and of the
cube ($3! \times 2^3$; the $24$ of determinant $+1$ its rotations, the
$24$ of $-1$ its reflections, told apart by the hand, a pseudoscalar of
the group), up to the law's two declared ties, the digital line's axis
order and the collision's Port order (\cite{beamlaw}, note 39 and
section 4; the apportioning's tie, by the row's age, breaks no axis
symmetry; this paper meets the first, Section~\ref{sec:model}); under the circle $\Z_{\Nphi}$
exactly on the turns between events and at the click only up to the
tables' rounding; and under the permutations of the families, each with
its columns of the family table carried along (Table~\ref{tab:f3}
gives, family by family, the group each declared quantity lives on).
The first tie is read
through the six faces' detectors in record 349 \cite{log}: one source
on five declared directions, its world mapped through all $48$; the
auditor's verdict, the label, the row's momentum and the hand
covariant, the Nodes crossed and the exit face covariant only under the
$8$ elements that keep the axis order, exact on every untied direction
(the mapped-back clicks and the escaped momentum equal the base under
those $8$ alone). The second tie is read the same way in record 359
\cite{log}: two transit singles meeting head-on, parked and permuted
again at every interval they dwell, come back equal to the base under
$16$ of the $48$ (the identity and one cyclic permutation of the axes,
with every sign) and differ under $32$, $16$ of them proper rotations,
the difference reaching the detector; the collision's outcome is
coupled to the flight's Link timing, and the covariance fails on the
collision as \cite{beamlaw}, section 4, states. A boost, which mixes a
step in space with a step in time, is not among the $48$: Lorentz's
symmetry is not a symmetry of the GameBoard but of the limit of its linear
block, the wave equation at $c$ (Section~\ref{sec:lorentz}). The $48$
are the GameBoard's stand-in for the rotation group; angular momentum is
conserved only up to the GameBoard's grain, and no Noether theorem for the map is
claimed \cite{log}. The name of the program counts them.

\begin{theorem}[the $24$ of the name]\label{th:group}
The maps of the six Ports onto themselves that send opposite Ports to
opposite Ports form the group of the signed permutations of the three
axes, of order $2^3 \cdot 3! = 48$. The determinant is a homomorphism of
this group onto $\{+1, -1\}$; its kernel, the rotations of the cube, has
index $2$ and order $24$, and is the group of the permutations of the
cube's four body diagonals; the other coset, the reflections, has $24$
elements too. The hand, the one pseudoscalar column of a row, which a
map $g$ sends to $\det(g)$ times itself, tells the $24$ rotations from
the $24$ reflections, as does an axial vector.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A map of the six Ports that keeps opposite Ports opposite permutes the
three axes ($3!$ choices) and then fixes a sign on each ($2^3$ choices),
and every such choice is realised by one signed permutation matrix; so
the group is the hyperoctahedral group $B_3$, the symmetry group of the
cube and of the octahedron of the Ports, of order $48$. The determinant
of a signed permutation matrix is $\pm 1$ and multiplicative, and it is
$-1$ on the reflection in one coordinate plane, so it is onto $\{+1,
-1\}$; the kernel of a homomorphism onto a group of two elements has
index $2$, so there are $24$ rotations and $24$ reflections. A rotation
of the cube permutes its four body diagonals; the only signed
permutations fixing all four diagonals as lines are the identity and
the central inversion, whose determinant is $-1$, so the action of the
kernel is faithful, and both groups having order $24$, the rotations are
the symmetric group on the four diagonals. A pseudoscalar column goes to
$\det(g)$ times itself by definition; series P's parity worlds with an
axis read exactly the $24$ improper elements as those under which the
image differs and none of the $24$ proper ones, and a hand without an
axis is mirror-equal under all $48$ \cite{beamlaw,log}. That the
six operations commute with the $48$ up to the declared ties is the
statement above, not part of this theorem.
\end{proof}

\part{What follows from the formula}\label{part:table}

\section{The table}\label{sec:table}

Every formula below is derived from the law of Section~\ref{sec:law} in
the derivation document of the archive \cite{derivations}, by the section
named, and checked against the recorded run named, from the register
\cite{register}; where the check is a pin written before a run not yet
made, the row says so. ``Reached'' means the known formula returns in
the continuum limit (proved there) and a recorded run agrees within the
stated grain (measured); where the run is pinned and not made the status
says ``pin''; ``different law'' means the law gives another formula,
stated so that a measurement can refute it (open, or measured against);
``not reached'' means no operation of the law produces it (open);
``input'' means the law takes it from the world file (assumed); ``new''
means a quantity of the law with no counterpart in known physics. The
first column gives the standing in one word: DERIVED, from
Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) and the inputs under the postulates, whether reached
exactly, in the limit, in form or pinned before its run; HYPOTHESIS, reached only under a named
hypothesis beside the law, not built; POSTULATE, assumed by the design (no row of this table: the postulates are P1 to P10);
INPUT, taken from the world file; DIFFERENT, a formula derived from
Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) whose leading term differs from nature's, refuted
or open;
NOT REACHED; OPEN. ``The law'' here is Eq.~(\ref{eq:map}) with its
components as decided (form B among them), and where the law as built
on main differs the status says so. The words follow the derivation's
inputs ledger (24.1 \cite{derivations}), which labels every place
physics enters the law. The route of every DERIVED row, its
operations, its limit, its pin and its reading, is the row of the same
formula, named more shortly, in the derivation record
(Tables~\ref{tab:recorda} to~\ref{tab:recordc}) and the section of
\cite{derivations} named in the derivation column, except the free
family's flux (in the Newton row's reading), the single-opening spread
(the uncertainty row's pin) and the continuum formulas
(Table~\ref{tab:continuum}); a row reached in form, as an identity of
readings or by reference says so in its status, and no row is a bare
``derived''. Every row stands on its derivation's section and its check; the rows
are written out here one by one as each passes review, those written out so
far in Sections~\ref{sec:newton}, \ref{sec:delay} and \ref{sec:gleason}.

\paragraph{Where the law's form stands above the known one.} Six
places, each with what the law adds and what the derivation holds (the
owner's order, record 352 \cite{log}); no claim goes beyond the
derivation \cite{derivations}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The exact square, $E^2/c^4 = m^2 + 3\,\mathbf p\cdot\mathbf p$ on the
record, compared and never rooted, with $m = N_l N_w M$ forced by the
Newtonian limit and $c^2 = 1/3$ from the flight (Section~\ref{sec:lorentz},
Figure~\ref{fig:square}). The energy--momentum relation of special
relativity, $E^2 = E_0^2 + p^2c^2$, is its content and is not claimed
as new; what the identity of the covariant
readings adds, a hypothesis beside the law and not built
(Section~\ref{sec:lorentz}), is the invariant as an integer condition,
$\Eint^2 \le E^2/c^4 < (\Eint+1)^2$, kept by comparisons with no real number
anywhere. What holds: the theorem of the derivation's 17,
that every quantity defined by covariant operations on the linear
block's limit transforms as the block does, so that ``above the known
relation'' is true of the linear block and of everything built from it alone, and,
for a body, exactly as true as its four readings are covariant; the
covariant readings are a hypothesis beside the law, not built.
\item The CHSH sum at finite $\Nphi$: a value at each $\Nphi$, $S(\Nphi, N_t)$, two-sided
about $2\sqrt2$, where quantum mechanics gives one value, $2\sqrt2$, and
the bound (the derivation's 6.2; Theorem~\ref{th:bell}); the one
prediction of Section~\ref{sec:differs}, $181/64$, is this value at
the powers of two from $512$ through $8192$; at $16384$ and $32768$ the
same rule gives $5793/2048$, and with the tables fixed the limit in
$\Nphi$ is $186034/65773$ (\cite{checks}, \texttt{s\_powers\_of\_two}).
\item Born's rule to the rung: a cell's probability a multiple of $1/\Nphi$,
within $1/\Nphi$ of the squared amplitude (each cumulative rung within
$1/(2\Nphi)$ of its cumulative weight), the continuum's rule its limit
(6.2; Definition~\ref{def:click}).
\item The uncertainty relation exact on the circle $\Z_{\Nphi}$ in its support
and entropic forms, Kennard's variance form the limit (22;
Section~\ref{sec:gleason}).
\item The entropy's accounting at the click: bits read plus bits erased
equal $\log_2 \Nphi$ per record (14), an identity of the outcome's coarse graining, the one non-bijective read located at the click,
the one read-out, with the cancel and the leaks of 14.2 beside it
(Theorem~\ref{th:bijection}; 25.1, rows 11 and 12), Boltzmann's count
reached exactly as the count, the kinetic $H$-theorem a reading not
made.
\item Gauss's law exact at every instant for a steady source on the GameBoard, not only in the
continuum: the amount crossing a closed surface per interval is the
release inside it, after the front (3.1 and 5.5; the two Gauss rows of
Table~\ref{tab:consequences}; Section~\ref{sec:continuum}, row 1, for
the continuity equation).
\end{enumerate}
What the paper wants and the derivation lacks is the derivation's
section 26 \cite{derivations}: ten formulas, each with its standing,
the identity or program that would reach it and its pin; none is
claimed here.

\section{Newton and Coulomb from the bilinear coupling}\label{sec:newton}

The first rows of Table~\ref{tab:consequences} are written out here from
the derivation's section 3 \cite{derivations} and the register's series
C \cite{register}: a plane of $121 \times 121$ Nodes with the third
axis periodic, one source of content $2^{24}$ holding a free family and
releasing on the six headings at the declared release rate $\eta = n/d
= 1/128$ units per self-creation per unit of content per direction (two
sources, of $2^{22}$ and $2^{20}$, in the third-law world); the two rows
released on the periodic axis land on the source's own Node at their
first step, are home, and are released again in six shares at the next
self-creation, so that at the fixed point the flux $q = 6 \times 2^{17}$
units enters the plane per interval in four in-plane beams of $3 \times
2^{16}$; probes of content $1$ at the radii $4$ to $40$, and of content
$1$, $4$ and $16$ at $r = 12$; twenty-one worlds, every reading pinned
before the run; the integers below are those of the run at the source
fingerprint named in Reproducibility, in label units (a later re-read along the
unit vector multiplies every push and momentum by $N_l = 64$ exactly,
the ratios unchanged). No number below is recomputed by this paper.

\paragraph{The operations.} Six operations, of three of Part~I's kinds
(the translation, the bilinear form, the Euclidean division), make the
whole of gravitation and electricity in the law. The free release (a
count against a wall): a body of content $M_B$ holding a free family
releases at each self-creation the carry of the count of rate $M_B\eta$
against the wall $1$ (the whole part of $M_B\eta$ per self-creation,
$M_B\eta$ in the mean) on every declared direction, so a fan of $K$
directions releases $q = K M_B\eta$ per interval while the source's
clock owes nothing. The walk (a translation): each unit walks its
digital line at the pace of Proposition~\ref{prop:pace}. The reading (a
bilinear form): the label flow $\mathbf a = \sum \mathrm{amount} \times
\mathbf u_D$ of the rows arriving at the reader's Node, $\mathbf u_D$
the label per unit along the direction $D$, the integer unit vector at
the scale $N_l$, $|\mathbf u_D| = N_l$ within $1.35$ percent on every
direction. The coupling (a bilinear form with a declared integer
matrix): per axis $\mathrm{push}_A = M_A (\rho_A\rho_B - 1)\,\mathbf a$,
the gravity column $-M_A \mathbf a$ and the charge column
$+\rho_A\rho_B M_A \mathbf a$, $\rho$ the declared charge per unit of
content of each family. The momentum (a translation): $\mathbf p_A
\leftarrow \mathbf p_A + \mathrm{push}_A$. The step (the Euclidean
division): one Link per $d_p/|p_a|$ self-creations on the axis $a$, the
wall $d_p = N_l N_w M_A + |p_a|$, $N_w$ the world's width; per interval, for a
body whose clock owes nothing, the speed on the axis is $|p_a|/(N_l N_w
M_A + |p_a|)$.

\paragraph{Gauss's law, exact.} The walk is a translation: a free unit
released inside a closed surface that no periodic axis crosses, with no
absorbing reader inside it and within the unit's lifetime, crosses the
surface once and never returns (no collision acts on a fan or on lone
beams), so once the front has passed, the amount crossing the surface
per interval equals the release inside it, exactly. Registered, on the
plane where the periodic axis's two rows are home and re-released as
said above: the flux through a square of half-width $h$ around the
source, over $q$, is $1.0000$ at $h = 4, 8, 12, 20, 40$, and the escape
through the faces $1.0000\,q$ (series C, item 5). Proved from the
definitions under those conditions; measured.

\paragraph{The inverse square as a shell mean.} On a beam the flow per
Node is the same at every Node of the digital line: along a beam the
field falls as $1/r^0$, and off every beam it is $0$ (registered: the
axial count $q/4$ per interval constant with $r$; a probe off both axes
reads nothing). The inverse square is the density of beams over a shell.
A shell at distance $r$ holds $\mathcal N(r)$ Nodes and the $K$ beams
cross it at $K$ of them, so the mean over the shell of what a body reads
is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:shell}
\langle a\rangle(r) = \frac{q\,N_l}{\mathcal N(r)} \to \frac{q\,N_l}{2\pi r}
\ \text{on the plane},\qquad \frac{q\,N_l}{4\pi r^2}\ \text{in space},
\end{equation}
as $\mathcal N(r) \to 2\pi r$ and $4\pi r^2$; the departure at finite $r$
is the GameBoard's count of Nodes on a shell, Gauss's circle problem,
$\mathcal N(r) = 2\pi r + O(r^\theta)$ with the exponent $\theta \le
131/208$ \cite{huxley2003}, so the shell mean's relative ripple vanishes
as $r^{\theta - 1}$. Registered (series C, item 5, nine radii; the
register's flow is the label flow over $N_l$, in amount units): the
count times $r$ over $q$ and the flow times $2\pi r$ over $q$ are
$r/\mathcal N(r)$ and $2\pi r/\mathcal N(r)$ to every printed digit,
$\mathcal N(r)$ counted on the GameBoard (Table~\ref{tab:rings}); the
design's expectation of a constant within ten percent was missed by the
flow at $r = 12, 16, 20$ ($1.109$, $0.898$, $1.122$) and by the count at
$r = 16$ ($0.143$) and in the ripple over $r \ge 8$ ($0.25$): the ring
counts $68$, $112$, $112$, not the law. Reached in
the shell mean, with the ripple named; on one Node the inverse square
exists only in the limit of every direction, and a fan declared as a
cube of directions has in that limit a cubic anisotropy of the
declaration, $3^{3/2}$ between a corner and a face centre, absent for a
fan declared within a ball.
