\paragraph{The decision.} The law stays the six operations and states
its prediction as declared, an unslowed counter in motion, as a row that
can fail against the muon in flight (Table~\ref{tab:nature}, rows 4a and
4b). The hypothesis built beside it for comparison was first the seventh
operation, an integer root at a declared grain slowing the counter by
$\gamma$ and contracting its bond by declaration, and is now the
covariant readings above, decided in its place on the day the theorem
was written \cite{log}. The derivation's grounds for naming them: they
need no operation beyond the six and give $E = mc^2$ and the electric part of the field where
the root gives the clock and the contraction by declaration and no
energy of motion. Both the law and the hypothesis are to be run against
the same pins, restated on the declared integers and as detector
readings (the derivation's 18.1 as amended): the muon of a J4 world of
shape $[201, 1, 1]$ at $p = 3640$ and $12\,856$ label units, its
electron product's click on the far face at $367$ and $345$ intervals
against $391$ at rest (re-derived with the drive's whole steps at
$368.2$, $345.2$ and $390.6$, within the two-interval tolerance), the
decay's $70.9$ and $125.2$ derived back by the flight table; the coasting star at its declared momentum, $z = 0.369 \pm
0.003$ ($0.315$ only on a re-declared momentum); the pair's round trips
withdrawn from this identity, since the contraction is not its to give;
the paper to state both readings when they exist. A third route of the same day, a slowing by the crowd a
moving body reads, is closed: the crossing count of a mover through an
isotropic crowd has the sphere mean exactly $1$ \cite{derivations}.
Whatever the runs show, the results of Part~III are unchanged, since no
row of its worlds carries a mass or a clock in motion.

\section{Literature and positioning}\label{sec:lit}

The click is a non-local step, forced by Bell's theorem because the
marginals are exact and $S > 2$ \cite{bell1964,chsh1969}; it is
parameter-dependent at fixed $u$ in the sense of the causal anatomy of
\cite{jarrett1984,shimony1986} as \cite{paper1} used it; no-signalling is
Theorem~\ref{th:marginals}, as it is a theorem of quantum mechanics
\cite{grw1980}.

't Hooft's cellular automaton interpretation \cite{thooft2016} keeps
determinism and meets Bell by superdeterminism; here the model is
deterministic given $u$, the settings are GameBoard clocks whose
periods, coprime to $\Nphi$ in the registered choosers' world, leave them
uncorrelated with $u$ (a period sharing a factor with $\Nphi$ would not, and
is not run), and the Bell value is met by an
explicit non-local click. Wolfram's hypergraph rewriting and Gorard's
multiway quantum mechanics \cite{wolfram2020,gorard2020} take the
branches of a multiway system as the superposition and a completion as
the collapse; here one GameBoard, one record with rows, one click with an
exact integer rule and finite tables whose consequences are computed.
The finite quantum kinematics of Weyl \cite{weyl1931} and Schwinger
\cite{schwinger1960}, the Weyl pair on $\Z_{\Nphi}$ and the unitary operator
bases, is the algebra the click's evaluation realises exactly; here $\Nphi$
is a declared grain of the law and the continuum's commutator its limit
(Section~\ref{sec:qm}). Lattice gases \cite{hpp1976,fhp1986} are exact
integer dynamics with the continuum law in the limit; the GameBoard is of that kind, with the
click's reading of one record added. Its pace $1/\sqrt3$ is the cubic
lattice's known number, the Courant bound of the discretised wave
equation \cite{cfl1928}, reached in Proposition~\ref{prop:pace} by the
Courant condition's own domain-of-dependence argument, and the sound
speed of the lattice Boltzmann models \cite{qian1992}, which stream every
population with one operator as the flight does; what the proposition
adds is the exact integer table at the bound, whose rounding's
anisotropy is a bound on the grain of the kind every lattice model of
light owes to the isotropy measurements \cite{nagel2015}. Where the
GameBoard parts from a discretised wave equation is dispersion: a wave
on the lattice has a group velocity that falls with the wave number and
a band edge, and the three-dimensional automata of
\cite{bialynicki1994,arrighi2019} are
dispersive with $v_g \to c$ only as $k \to 0$, while a row here is the
pure-shift case, a walker on a digital line with one pace at every
phase rate (Remark~\ref{rem:nodispersion}), so the time-of-flight tests
\cite{abdo2009} bound a wave scheme's Link by its order and its
direction and this one's not at all. Quantum cellular automata and
quantum lattice gases \cite{bialynicki1994,meyer1996,arrighi2019} are
local unitary rules on complex amplitudes; here no complex number is on the GameBoard, the split is an exact integer isometry, and the tables'
unitarity holds within $361/65536$ and is computed, not assumed. Bohm's
theory and quantum equilibrium \cite{bohm1952,dgz1992} take the hidden
position distributed as $|\psi|^2$ (from typicality); here a uniform $u$
and the ruler do the same work by inverse-transform sampling, a
different distribution, one integer per record with a table for the
guidance, and, like Bohm's, non-local at the pair. Nelson's stochastic
mechanics \cite{nelson1966} is stochastic where nothing here is, once
$u$ is fixed. Objective collapse \cite{grw1986,bassi2013} has real
discrete events with a free rate and length; the click is triggered by
the record's completion at the apparatus and has neither, and nothing
collapses spontaneously (a pair stays entangled without maintenance). In
the classification of ontological models \cite{spekkens2007,hs2010} the
rows' amplitudes and $u$ are ontic, so the model is $\psi$-ontic; its
non-locality is Bell's (Section~\ref{sec:causal}). On Tsirelson's bound
\cite{tsirelson1980,pr1994}, its finite-precision form \cite{bhz2005}
and finite precision in foundations \cite{meyer1999,kent1999}: Theorem~\ref{th:bell} puts the model's
$S(\Nphi)$ on both sides of $2\sqrt2$ with the marginals exact, a
no-signalling box of the Popescu--Rohrlich kind at finite $\Nphi$, of a size
a Bell measurement of precision $0.0005$ already constrains
(Section~\ref{sec:bell}). The single-quantum build-up of fringes
\cite{tonomura1989,grangier1986}, GHZ \cite{ghsz1990,mermin1990,pan2000}
and interaction-free measurement \cite{ev1993} are the experiments the
measurements of Section~\ref{sec:measure} are named for; none of them is
reproduced in its own geometry, and the two-slit pattern here is a sparse
fan's spots.

\section*{Appendix: how every formula was reached}\label{sec:record}

Every formula of this paper was reached in the same three steps, and
the appendix records them per formula so that a reader can see where a
step depends on nothing but the law, and where it does not. (1)
\emph{The pin.} The rule is written as its operations on the state
(Part~I) and its closed form is taken in the limit named; from the
closed form, and from nothing else, an expectation is derived by a host
script or by hand and written to the register before any run (the
world's expectation file, or the register's paragraph, written first)
\cite{register}. (2) \emph{The limit.} The closed form is compared with
the classical formula in the limit (the Link to zero, many rows, large
$\Nphi$, small momentum), the order of the expansion and the grain's error
term stated where the derivation states them \cite{derivations}. (3)
\emph{The run.} The simulator runs the world, which declares only the
family table, the grain and the initial state (the architecture's
contract forbids a formula string, an expression tree or a callback in
any payload \cite{architecture}); a reading tool reads the detector's
record and compares it with the pin, inside or outside, and an outside
pin is reported and never moved. The independence is structural where
it holds: the engine contains the six operations and its tables; the
classical formula lives in the derivation and in the reading tool, not
in the run. Two places need the caveat. The flight's table $T_D =
\lfloor\sqrt{3|D|^2 N_l^2}\rfloor$ is the integer rule itself, so a run
on it establishes that the engine is its closed form and no physical
law, as the register says of series Q; and the phase circle's cosine
table, $\cos(2\pi d/\Nphi)$ with $d$ the phase difference in steps and $\Nphi$ the
circle's phases, rounded to $1/256$ and computed once at load as
immutable law data, a declared input of the law (Part~I, ``What is put
in''; record 328 \cite{log}), is the rotation of Part~III, so the click
rows' runs carry that ingredient of their closed form. The collision table is
generated from its class rule at load and holds no formula. Not every
row has a pin: where a number was read after its run by a host script
(the information cost, the entropy, the wall's re-read of the thrown
stars), the tables say so, and the four labels are applied as the
caption states: a reading inside a pin written first is measured; a
result of the derivation alone is proved; a pin without its run, or a
number read after the run, is assumed or open, never measured.
Tables~\ref{tab:recorda} and~\ref{tab:recordb} are the paper's rows of
the derivation map (\cite{derivations}, section 21), one row per
formula; since this round the map itself carries the pin before the run
as a column beside the run, with the same words where none exists.

\section*{Appendix: the families, the sets, the groups and the vectors}\label{sec:families}

Every number in this appendix is a declaration of the register, an
input under P8, and none is a measurement: no run made them. They are
read from the one source, the definitions file
\texttt{examples/events/entities/families.json} and the worlds under
\texttt{examples/events/}, by the mathematician's host script, whose
audit found that the paper carried the family table's schema and never
its rows \cite{familiesaudit} (the owner's word of 2026-09-21, record
358 \cite{log}). The keys are the engine's \cite{engine}: $h$ the
quantum, $0$ for a free family and $1$ or more for a paid one (the kind
is derived from it, never declared); the charge, per unit of content for
a free family (an integer, or a fraction $n/d$, a winding on the circle)
and the whole charge per unit of amount for a paid one; the strong
column with its value and sign, the further column of the one coupling
beside gravity and charge; the lifetime $L$, the age at which a row of
the family clicks on the border of that name, for ever when unset; the
phase circle, yes for a phase on $\Z_{\Nphi}$ and no for a phase-less family
that turns never; the hand, the pseudoscalar bit $\pm 1$ that the $48$
read by the determinant, none when unset. A world may declare a family
beyond the definitions, or with other values than the shipped ones;
those declarations are the register's declared variants of that world
(Table~\ref{tab:f1b}), read by the same law, not a second law. The
count: $75$ families in $28$ entities (the sets) in the definitions, and
$21$ declarations of the worlds that differ from them.

\paragraph{F4, the state vector per kind.} What a row and a body carry,
for every family alike, with the per-family differences in
Table~\ref{tab:f1} (\cite{engine}; the vector form's statement of the
state \cite{lawvector}):
\begin{itemize}
\item A row: its Node ($\Z^3$ on the box), its direction (a primitive
vector of the fan $\mathcal F$), its age ($\Z$), its phase ($\Z_{\Nphi}$), its number
(the family), its amount and its content per unit; under a record its
identity, its label (bits, one per arm), its multiplicity and its birth
phase $u$ ($\Z_{N_u}$); its momentum the label amount times $\mathbf u_D$ at
the scale $N_l$, a vector of label units.
\item A body, a measured event: its Node, its content $M$, its momentum
vector $\mathbf p$ (label units), its charge per column ($\rho M$), its
held content per family, its table of counts (one accumulator per
count: the drive, the clock, the wheel, the action) and its set of
Nodes.
\item The six verbs on these, the six operations of P3: the
translation, the bilinear form, the group-ring addition on $\Z[\Z_{\Nphi}]$,
the permutation (the $48$), the evaluation (the tables) and the
division toward zero with the remainder kept (Euclidean for a non-negative accumulator).
\end{itemize}

\section*{Appendix: glossary and symbols}\label{sec:glossary}

The terms and symbols of this paper are those of the program's one
glossary, \texttt{docs/TERMINOLOGY.md} at the commit
\texttt{6d57c7a2} \cite{terminology}, which names every canonical term
once with the note, section or record that owns it, lists the
identities (the law and the hypotheses beside it) and the retired
words; where this paper spells a symbol differently from the glossary
the glossary's spelling is given. Terms: a \emph{Node} is a location
of the GameBoard, the cubic box of Nodes joined by \emph{Links} through
six \emph{Ports}; a \emph{row} is an event in transit, a message; a
\emph{body} is a measured event, a record with counts; a \emph{record}
is the rows of one quantum born at one lamp; a \emph{click} is a paid
row's units merged into a body's record, and the record's one read-out
of Definition~\ref{def:click}, its gather, is what Part~III calls the
click; a \emph{family} is a row of the family table; the \emph{crowd} of
a reader is everything present at its Node but its own number (the
arrivals of every family and number and the body there), and the crowd
audit of Part~I counts the six neighbouring Nodes' arrivals with it; a
\emph{pin} is an expectation written before its run; the word
\emph{lattice} in this paper names $\Z^3$ as a mathematical object, the
lattice gases of the literature and the lattice Gleason, and the
physical thing is the GameBoard. The kind of every symbol follows the
notation rule, a scalar plain, a vector in bold lowercase, a matrix or
an operator in bold uppercase, except where the table says a symbol is
written plain.

\section*{Reproducibility}
The code, the worlds of series L with their expectation file written
before the runs, the reading tool that replays a run's register into its
world list, the check scripts of the exact computations with their
outputs, the runs' summary and the two figure scripts are archived
\cite{zenodo}; the register entry of series L carries every run's world,
duration and source fingerprint, and a table beside the manuscript maps
each number of this paper to its source. The project's declared
interpreter is Python 3.14; no floating point is in any physical module;
the runs take seconds each (the register's durations). The register's runs carry the
fingerprint \texttt{ff5c382d672f} (the gate worlds re-run after the
gate's fix with the same integers); the figures' runs carry
\texttt{731d0f56c9f9}, the fingerprint of \texttt{figures/summary.json}
\cite{checks} on the paper's tree; every integer is equal. The two
cone worlds of L7 carry \texttt{4bf55a62e6fd}, the tree of the one click.
The archived version cited at submission is the tagged release after the
merge, one tree whose history holds the three trees named above and the
log records this paper cites; it replaces the concept DOI below, and its
code no longer has the world key. Every record, note, design and
register row this text cites is on the tree it was written on (main
merged at \texttt{6b707a53}); the far lamp's rows 11a to 11c rest on a
pin whose run is not made. The commits the body refers to here (the
precedents' counsel: the record in the appendix, not in the captions
\cite{precedents}): the confrontation register at its base
\texttt{88d843ef}, row 8c added at \texttt{d1fe2712}, rows 11a to 11c
at \texttt{8b13ceeb} merged at \texttt{01bb782a}, row 2a restated at
\texttt{d66ac8b6}, row 9 restated to PASS at \texttt{71e15626}; series
C's integers at the source fingerprint \texttt{703f9427}; the crowd-clock
worlds run on main \texttt{119fd9b}, the cluster's and the reader's on
\texttt{d8cb46e}; the program's glossary at \texttt{6d57c7a2}; the
replications of round 1 re-run on the tree at \texttt{10a9ac6}
\cite{replications}.

The simulator's code, the design documents, the check scripts and the
drafts of this manuscript were produced with AI coding agents working
under the author's direction and review; the author verified every
number against the archived runs and is responsible for the whole text.
No AI system is an author. [The tool and version are named here at
submission.]
